Demo: AURORA_DEMO_GLOW_LAYERS=1|shell with AURORA_DEBUG_GLOW=1 (pink layer with glow 0.02 on a skin with glow 0.02: 0.02 under the layer, against 0.035 before in shell mode). Validated by the human on Agni on 2026-10-13: the glow of an avatar with glowing layers displays as in Firestorm
- ✅ GLTF material overrides (LLGLTFMaterialList::applyOverrideMessage, LLGLTFMaterial::applyOverrideLLSD / applyOverride): GenericStreamingMessage 0x4175, LLSD notation payload (new parser in aurora-llsd, like LLSDNotationParser); each message replaces the object's overrides, kept apart like LL's region cache (an override can arrive before the object); rendered material = asset + override (textures, base colour, emissive, metal, roughness, alpha, double-sided, repeats / offsets / rotations per map); KHR transform specific to each map (normal, metal-roughness, emissive); the face colour no longer applies to PBR faces (LLFace::getGeometryVolume). AOC2 object cache with the overrides (replayed on a cache hit, like objects_*_extras.slec); AOC1 caches are dropped once so that the simulator resends the overrides. "Describe" also writes the PBR transforms and the overrides. Cause of the floor of Tordangle Harbor (box of 64 × 12.5 m, PBR materials at 1 × 1). Demo: AURORA_DEMO_PBR_OVERRIDE=1. Verified on Agni on 2026-10-09: herringbone floor identical to Firestorm
- ✅ Texture animation (llSetTextureAnim) like LLViewerTextureAnim::animateTextures and LLVOVolume::animateTextures: modes ON, LOOP, REVERSE, PING_PONG, SMOOTH, ROTATE, SCALE, frame grid, start / length / rate (negative included), single face or ALL_SIDES (face out of range = all); the animation replaces the face's rotation, repeats or offsets, the rest comes from the texture entry; normals / specular of legacy materials follow the diffuse texture; PBR faces: before the KHR transform of each map (texture_matrix0 of textureUtilV.glsl). Clock like Firestorm: a non-smooth animation restarts at each full update of the object (llSetText restarts it too in Firestorm), a smooth one continues. Modern: parameters written once into the face's GPU record (256 bytes), evaluated by the vertex shaders (scene, masked prepass, shadows, reflections, probes, impostors) with the renderer's clock; no CPU work per frame. Demo: AURORA_DEMO_TEXANIM=1 (AURORA_CAPTURE_FRAMES accepts several frames). Deliberate differences: small faces (< MIN_TEX_ANIM_SIZE) stay animated; at rate 0, a PBR face is not animated (Firestorm freezes the 1st frame in the texture entry, which PBR ignores). To verify on the grid: road under the motorbike at Yikes, waterfalls, screens, rotating textures; validated by the human on Agni on 2026-10-09 (animated road under the motorbike at Yikes, like Firestorm)
- ✅ PBR under a classic sky: environment specular added like pbrBaseLight (pbrIbl), with the sky reflection for lack of probes, then the probes (arcade floor close to Firestorm)
- ✅ complexity, continued: triangles of prims at each LOD (getLoDTriangleCounts, path and profile points at details 1 / 1.5 / 2.5 / 4); sculpted prims at 4 triangles like LL (no cost data); surface area limit of attachments (RenderAutoMuteSurfaceAreaLimit 1000 m², area 1 per prim or that of the sculpted prims' grid × the largest dimension, ignored without a complexity limit); "Limit applied": to everyone / except friends / friends only (RenderAvatarComplexityMode); per-avatar exceptions in the right-click menu (normal display / always fully / never, saved) with the avatar's complexity; complexity in name tags (Interface: on, silhouettes only, mine optional, like FSTagShowARW) + area when it is the area that exceeds the limit; impostors: beyond the N nearest avatars ("Fully rendered avatars"), image of the avatar and its attachments taken from the camera (atlas of 8×8 cells of 256 px, 2 images per displayed frame, refreshed according to distance, angle and movement), drawn on a panel lit and hazed like the scene; before they were hidden
- ✅ impostors faithful to the avatar drawn in full (reported on 2026-10-12 at KB: impostors "all grey and a bit buggy"): the card takes the haze of the classic sky at its real distance, like the rest of the scene (before: the generic fog, much lighter under a hazy sky — with a distance multiplier of 37, impostors washed out to blue-grey from 10 m), and the photo is taken without haze; an avatar still loading stays a cloud (before: photographed half loaded, in its cloud); beyond the complexity limit, photo of the silhouette alone, without attachments, in the same grey as when drawn in full (photo retaken as soon as the avatar crosses the limit); the textures of impostor avatars are requested at the fineness the photo shows (before: with no size on screen, they stayed at their coarsest level as long as the avatar had not been among the nearest). In demo: `AURORA_DEMO_SKY=<gamma>,<haze>` with `AURORA_DEMO_CROWD` and `AURORA_MAX_AVATARS` (the demo region is then EEP: its sky was no longer displayed). Validated on Agni on 2026-10-12 (KB, hazy local sky, SMAA, 16 fully rendered avatars)
- ✅ avatars still loading: like Firestorm (updateIsFullyLoaded / getRezzedStatus / processFullyLoadedChange), an avatar is loaded when its bakes are downloaded and its attachments have arrived and been built (meshes included), after a delay of 3 to 15 s the first time (×1.25 as an impostor), 60 s at most; meanwhile: particle cloud (motion from Firestorm's cloud.xml, Aurora colours violet → turquoise, soft default particle) and a thin violet → turquoise progress bar under the name. "Loading avatars" option: cloud + bar / bar only / nothing. Offline: no cloud (demo avatars have no bakes), AURORA_DEMO_CLOUD=1 to test it. Fixed on 2026-10-08: avatars stayed as clouds for ~60 s because the bakes declared but unused (bakes on mesh: arms, auxiliaries) were never downloaded; only the textures actually used count, attachments = expected geometry, silhouettes excluded, textures of loading avatars given priority; log "avatar … still loading after 20 s" with what is missing. Delay fixed: once everything is loaded, 3 s the first time (1 s afterwards) like processFullyLoadedChange — LL's 3 to 15 s only apply during loading (before: up to 15 s of extra cloud, seen on the grid: complete avatars 123/123 and 457/457 still clouds at 20 s). Bar: 90% = parts ready / known, 10% = final delay, never goes backwards. Confirmed on Agni after the fix: avatars displayed in 6 to 9 s
- ✅ attachments awaited like Firestorm (updateIsFullyLoaded): another avatar is displayed when as many attachments have arrived as the simulator lists with its appearance (AttachmentBlock of AvatarAppearance, mSimAttachments), within the 60 s limit (before: 2 s without a new object, blindly — too early for an avatar whose objects arrive slowly, 2 s too many for the others); once the count is reached, it is no longer compared, and a new list of a different size restarts it as long as the avatar has not been displayed. Our avatar is not compared with this list (`!isSelf()`): displayed 3 s after its bakes and the geometry of its objects have arrived, instead of 5 s (demo AURORA_DEMO_CLOUD=self: "(own) shown after 3.0 s"). An avatar for which the simulator lists nothing does not wait for objects, as in Firestorm. Log: "worn objects 12 of 14 listed". No cloud again for an avatar already displayed, contrary to what this line used to say: Firestorm no longer re-evaluates an avatar once loaded (idleUpdateLoadingEffect only calls updateIsFullyLoaded again while mFullyLoaded is false). Validated on Agni on 2026-10-13: our avatar displayed in 4.1 s at login; in a busy region, 32 avatars displayed with their complete list ("21 of 21 listed"…), and at 20 s about fifteen still clouds because the simulator had not sent all their attachments ("0 of 12 listed"): they wait up to the 60 s limit, as in Firestorm (before: displayed without their objects)
- ✅ Cost of lights without a projector: the point-light loop is once again the one from before the projectors, which have their own (empty when the list contains none); +0.09 ms of GPU per frame brought back to +0.01 ms, within measurement noise, in the load scene `AURORA_DEMO_LIGHTS=64` + `AURORA_DEMO_CROWD=40` (0.98 ms before the projectors, 0.99 ms with the projectors and their shadows, 5 alternated passes); image of `AURORA_DEMO_PROJECTOR` unchanged. Cost of projector shadows in this demo scene: 0.845 → 0.855 ms of GPU per frame

## Optimisation

- ✅ Detailed profile in the log (AURORA_PROFILE=1) to measure on the grid: one "perf summary" line per second (fps, mean / p95 / max time, CPU time per step of the frame — events, camera, poses, scene sync, lists, streaming, interface, rendering… — and per step of the renderer `r_*` — passes, egui, finish, submission, waiting for the frame —, GPU time per item `g_*`, draws and draw commands, objects resynchronised / rebuilt, animated avatars, bytes of records and palettes sent to the GPU, texture and geometry memory) and "perf settings" when the settings change. Used on Agni on 2026-10-09 (RTX 4090, 2560×1369): large shop in MSAA 24 fps (45 ms) with a GPU at 7 ms → limited by the CPU
- ✅ Perf, from this reading (one PR per point, before / after measurement on Agni); the points still open are in "To do" above:
  - ✅ scene sync: 10,000 to 21,000 objects resynchronised every frame (every child prim was treated as "moving"), 5,000 to 7,000 rebuilt per frame, 6 to 9 MB of records resent to the GPU per frame → 9 to 15 ms. Fixed: a child prim is only reprocessed if one of its ancestors moves (extrapolated velocity, acceleration, rotation), is an avatar, a rigged mesh, or has just been updated (Scene::follows_motion, computed before the loop); fully transparent faces (without a record) no longer trigger a full rebuild every frame (comparison with the built faces, no longer with the drawn faces); a record whose matrix has not changed is no longer resent. 4 tests added. Measured on Agni on 2026-10-09 (same shop): objects resynchronised 9,900–13,300 → 2,700 per frame (2,000 when quiet, mostly attachments), rebuilds 5,100–5,700 → 300 while loading / 0 when quiet, 6.5–9 MB → 2.6 MB sent to the GPU, sync 8.8–11 ms → 3.2–4.7 ms; validated visually by the human
  - ✅ event-driven scene sync (Agni reading: sync ~3.4 ms per frame, ~2,000 attached prims repositioned every frame, several loops over all the objects): no more walk over all the objects every frame (follows_motion pre-computation, main loop, garbage collection of the geometries, recount of the pending geometries). Sets kept up to date (scene/sync_sets.rs): objects that move by themselves (velocity, avatars, our agent, rigged meshes) and everything that follows them, found through the child lists (links, unlinks, attachments, detachments without bookkeeping); modified objects, noted by ObjectStore (upsert, get_mut; the sync clears its flags through get_mut_untracked), with their whole linkset if they have been moved; users of materials at a new generation; pending geometries revisited only when they can make progress; LOD slice (each object every 24 frames, only the frame's slice is walked, and it serves as a safety net); unused geometries freed through a queue (last release), counter of the pending geometries kept as things happen. Placement (transform, LOD, bounds, matrix) computed in parallel with rayon (scene/sync_plan.rs), level by level of the parent chains: each avatar, then each attachment root, is placed only once per frame and its prims take its transform; update of the matrix alone applied afterwards (only 64 bytes written per record, RecordStore::set_model), the rest (geometry, faces, avatars) by the full sync as before. Poses: signature of the skeleton recomputed only when an object of the avatar changes or a mesh metadata arrives (still every 2 s and at a new appearance), members of the skeleton found without going up the chain of each prim. Fixed along the way: a linked sculpt kept its texture wait and was resynchronised every frame (and counted as "loading" for the avatar clouds). New scenario AURORA_DEMO_CROWD=<n> (n animated avatars wearing 8 linksets of 7 prims, + the rigged mesh with AURORA_DEMO_ANIMESH). Demo (release, RTX 4090, medians): crowd of 40 avatars (2,337 objects, 2,286 repositioned per frame) sync 1.23–1.33 → 0.62–0.72 ms, poses 0.53–0.57 → 0.33–0.35 ms, 184–198 → 210–246 fps; with the rigged meshes sync 1.27–1.39 → 0.65–0.70 ms; 9,000 motionless cubes sync 0.57–0.61 → 0.21–0.22 ms; small demo unchanged. Before / after screenshots identical apart from animations and particles, clean GPU validation. 14 tests (sets, links, motion, attachments, pending geometries, LOD slice, transform cache, skeleton members, skeleton invalidation). To verify on Agni: busy shop and place with many avatars (attachments that follow the animations, objects that move / rotate, modified linksets, teleports) — validated on Agni on 2026-10-09 (BackBone Main Store, ~17,000 objects: sync 3.4 → 1.25 ms, poses 0.5 → 0.28 ms per frame; Yikes at 299 fps; attachments, avatars, sitting and teleports correct)
  - ✅ texture streaming: 4 to 16 ms per frame, proportional to the number of textures (per-texture work every frame in TextureStreamer::update). Fixed: no more copy of the keys nor second lookup, cache paths built only for the first read from disk (before: two PathBuf + format! per texture and per frame); MeshStreamer::update no longer clones the data / header of the ready meshes when no LOD is requested. Measured on Agni on 2026-10-09 (same shop): streaming 5–10 ms → 0.3 ms per frame; MSAA 4x + high shadows: 24 → 45–53 fps since the first reading (with the scene sync); textures checked by eye against Firestorm. Remaining gap: Firestorm 129 fps at the same place
  - ✅ encoder.finish(): 6 to 10 ms, bindless array always filled to 16,384 entries that wgpu tracks at every pass (wgpu-core track/texture.rs merge_bind_group: each element, at every pass that binds the group). Fixed: group bound only up to the last used slot, in steps of 256 (PARTIALLY_BOUND_BINDING_ARRAY; without this option, the whole array as before), new textures in the lowest free slot, immediate rebuild when a slot exceeds the bound length. Demo: finish 2.7–3.7 → 0.41 ms, GPU validation with no error; Agni (shop, ~9,000 textures): 7.5–8 → 5.8 ms. The 3 shadow cascades are also rendered in a single pass into a 2×2 atlas (viewport + matrix per cascade, coordinates clamped to the centre of the edge texels of each cell = same result as the previous clamp-to-edge): the texture group is bound once instead of three times; Agni (same shop, SMAA, high shadows): 5.8 → 4.9 ms, frame 17 ms (59 fps, 24 at the first reading), shadows and textures validated by eye by the human on 2026-10-09. ~4.9 ms left: see the culling line below
  - ✅ texture memory: ~14,600 uncompressed textures; after a setting change the GPU goes from 4 to 85 ms per frame (render targets recreated outside video memory, very probably) → enforce the budget (lower the distant / hidden textures like LL). Cause in the code: the budget only acted on new loads (0 to 2 levels fewer), an already loaded texture was never reduced, and the limit was a share of the card's size read at startup, without counting the render targets or the other programs. Done (`scene/texture_budget.rs`, `aurora-render/src/vram.rs`): target recomputed four times per second = the "Texture memory" setting, or less when the rest of what the viewer holds on the card (render targets, geometry: bytes of the live textures and buffers, wgpu's counters) does not leave that room under Firestorm's target (budget − 512 MB, 80% at most, 768 MB at least, LLViewerTexture::updateClass), the budget being the one the driver grants the viewer at that moment (VK_EXT_memory_budget). Pressure steps 0 to 7: without pressure nothing is reduced, like Firestorm; 1 brings each texture down to its on-screen size; 2 to 7 then remove half a level at a time, up to two levels, out of view first (Firestorm's bias divides by 16 at most). The step goes up after 1 s above the target once the previous one has been applied, and goes back down below 90% of the target only if the textures would still fit one step lower (deliberate difference: Firestorm's bias goes back down as soon as memory is free, the textures reload and memory fills up again, in a loop). Reduction on the GPU by copying the levels already present to a smaller layer (`TextureTable::shrink`, like LLImageGL::scaleDown), without re-decoding, 3,000 per second at most (8 to 512 per frame), the largest first, the pages that have to be created for them spread over the frames; half a level of margin between reduction and reload; out-of-view textures keep what their distance asks for (no blur when turning the camera); bakes, HUDs, interface images, sculpties, sky and terrain never reduced; under pressure, unused textures evicted after 5 s (45 s otherwise) and pages compacted every 50 ms whatever their fill, to free those that the reduced textures have left. `AURORA_TEXTURE_BUDGET_MB` forces the target; "Texture budget" line in the Performance window; log `texture memory: pressure a -> b`. Demo (`AURORA_DEMO_STREAM=3000`, avatar in the middle of the objects, RTX 4090, dev profile): pages 4,786 MB without a budget → 202 MB under a forced 512 MB (step 1; difference between the two screenshots smaller than that between two screenshots without a budget), 55 to 83 MB under 96 MB (steps 1 to 4 or 5 depending on the trial, then back to 0); neither `stream` nor `s_maintain` in `max:` over the seconds recorded during the reductions; without pressure, memory unchanged (4,786 MB). Tests: target, steps, wanted and kept levels, list of reductions, compaction, and a GPU test (`cargo test -p aurora-render shrink -- --ignored`); `check.ps1` green after merging main in (#152 to #154: 1,170 tests passed, 17 ignored), the 10 GPU tests of the renderer passed; demos under 96 MB, `AURORA_DEMO_TEXTURES=9000` with churn, `AURORA_DEMO_STREAM=3000,3000,leave` and standard demo with `AURORA_GPU_VALIDATION=1`, with no error. Not reproduced here: the overflow itself (85 ms), which requires filling the card's 24 GB. First trial by the human on Agni on 2026-10-12 (BackBone Main Store, 16,525 objects, 8,879 textures, version with four steps of a whole level): 4,755 MB of pages without pressure for a target of 16,896 MB; "Texture memory" at 1,024 MB: 2,580 MB at step 1, then 908 to 970 MB stable, under the target in 10 s and without reloads in a loop. Three defects read in the log of that trial, fixed since: (a) 3.9 GB of freed pages lowered by only 1 GB the memory the driver counts against the viewer (wgpu's allocator keeps its blocks as long as a resource remains in them); the rest was counted from that figure, which lowered the target by as much at each reduction (14,295 MB instead of 16,896 after the trial) → rest counted from the live resources; (b) 1,037 MB for 1,024 cost one more whole level in view for 5 s (812 MB) → steps of half a level; (c) frames of 10 to 14 ms instead of 6 during the reductions (`r_resources` up to 9.9 ms and `r_submit` 4 ms in `max:`, page creations and destructions very probably) → created pages spread over the frames, then, since the merge of the pages created off the main thread (#154), requested from the page thread like those of the uploads: the reduction waits for its page. Second trial by the human on Agni on 2026-10-12, fixed version, validated ("sharp textures, and it works"): same place, 9,047 textures, "Texture memory" at 1,024 MB while the shop was finishing loading: 3,309 → 1,133 MB in 8 s (steps 1 to 5), then 809 to 947 MB, step 5 / 7 (one level fewer in view, two out of view) with a 5 → 4 → 5 round trip in 20 s when the camera moved; during the reduction, 135 to 200 fps, p95 7 to 9.6 ms, longest frame 8.5 to 14 ms (`r_resources` up to 5.6 ms); when the automatic setting is restored the target returns to 16,896 MB while the driver still counts 4,092 MB against the viewer for 1,859 MB of resources. Anti-aliasing changed in the shop under 1,024 MB (SMAA → MSAA 2x → SMAA): GPU time 2.6 → 3.4 → 2.6 ms, with no spike. Still not replayed: the original overflow (85 ms), video memory being far from full during these trials (4 GB out of 23)
  - ✅ interface (3 to 5 ms per frame): the hover cursor called pick_world every frame, which copied the whole screen depth (~14 MB) and waited for the GPU. Fixed: read of a single texel by a compute pass (pick.rs), asynchronous for the hover (answer 1 to 2 frames later), blocking only for clicks (4 bytes); profile split up (ui_prep, ui, hover, tessellate). Agni: interface 3–5 ms → ~2.1 ms (egui 0.5, hover 1.4, tessellation 0.2). Hover afterwards: ray / world bounding sphere test (kept by the sync) before each object's transform, matrix inverse and box test; same filter for clicks (pick_at_filtered); Agni: hover 1.4 → 0.5 ms, interface ~1.3 ms in total, shop 62–68 fps; cursors, clicks, menus, media and sitting validated by the human on 2026-10-09
  - ✅ quick wins from the rendering audit: frame recorded in 3 encoders finished in parallel (wgpu's finish() replays and tracks each command; only a shared lock is taken); separate thread pool for background tasks (decoding, disk): the frame no longer waits behind them in its par_iter calls; records of a motionless avatar not resent (animations go through the bone palette); reclassification of the alpha of all the faces only when a texture changes class or faces are rebuilt; upload blocks of 64 records instead of 256; palettes sent only for the skeletons animated in the frame; light list drawn from a set of light-emitting objects instead of all the objects. Demo: records 90 → 28 KB per frame. Agni (same shop): 66–74 fps (62–68 before), finish 4.9 → 4.4 ms, records 2.6 → 0.8 MB, palettes 480 → 84 KB per frame; AO, attachments, lights, textures and teleport validated by the human on 2026-10-09. The parallelism of the encoders brings little (~0.5 ms): texture tracking dominates in the scene's encoder and at submission → texture pages
  - ✅ texture pages (encoder.finish() and submission): textures of the same size and same number of mips share a 2D array texture ("page", one layer each); the bindless array holds the pages (a few dozen to a few hundred) instead of one entry per texture, and the shaders read the location (page, layer) of each slot in a buffer (`tex_loc`): the slots stay stable for the scene and the streaming, the group is only rebuilt when a page is created / deleted. First page of a size ~2 MB, then half of what that size already occupies, up to 32 MB; layer given back on release, empty page deleted, resolution increase towards a layer of the page of the new size (in place if the size does not change), media updated in its layer, page filled to a quarter at most emptied into the other pages of its size (GPU copy, at most one per second). Identical image: same texels, same mips, same sampler; before / after screenshots pixel-identical apart from particles and animations (demo, media, animesh, load test). Load test: AURORA_DEMO_TEXTURES=9000 (9,000 cubes, one texture each, 8 sizes including one that is not a power of two, arriving in low resolution then complete). Demo in dev profile with GPU validation: finish 7.4 → 3.3 ms (the rest is that of the same scene with 8 textures: cost of the draws, no longer of the textures), submission 0.58 → 0.21 ms, GPU 6.3 → 4.8 ms; 51 pages, 300 MB allocated for 243 MB of textures; small demo: finish 1.0–1.2 → 0.65 ms. Without PARTIALLY_BOUND_BINDING_ARRAY: 2,048 pages bound at most. New readings: `texture_pages` and `pages_mb` in "perf summary", "Texture pages" line of the performance panel. Also verified by three GPU tests (`cargo test -p aurora-render -- --ignored`: round trip, random trial with compaction, sampling through the bind group) and by AURORA_DEMO_TEXTURES_CHURN=1 (cubes removed then put back: layers freed, compaction, slots reused). Diagnostics: upload refused by the renderer reported, textures in use never loaded listed every 30 s, faces of attachments whose texture does not show detailed in the report of nearby avatars. To validate on Agni: textures after streaming and resolution increases, media, teleports, page memory. Agni (2026-10-09, large shop, ~9,900 textures in 411 pages, +3% memory): finish 4.4 → 0.84 ms, frame 10.7 ms (~97 fps); textures, media and teleport validated by the human
  - ✅ shadows: visible step in sharpness between cascades (3×3 filtering in texels, hence a different blur in each cascade; Firestorm softens in screen space, RenderShadowBlurSize) → filtering of fixed width in metres and fade between cascades over a transition band. Done: 4×4 taps spread over a penumbra of 3 cm half-width in all the cascades (spacing bounded between ½ and 2 texels to stay smooth; the far cascade, with texels of ~7 cm, stays blurrier), fade towards the next cascade over the last 15% of each cascade (a cascade that does not cover the point does not enter the blend). Validated by the human on Agni on 2026-10-09 (Yikes, side by side with Firestorm): no more step, stable shadows
  - ✅ shadows that jumped while the EEP sun rotates (all the shadows moved in jerks, camera still; Firestorm makes them glide): the texel grid was anchored to the region's origin (~200 m), so it swept the shadows at up to ~12 texels/s near the camera; now anchored to a cell the size of the cascade around the camera (shadow_anchor): 20 to 80 times slower, stability kept when the camera moves. Validated by the human on Agni on 2026-10-09 (Yikes)

## Caches

- ✅ Rolling disk cache (LRU): textures, meshes, materials, animations (shared between regions), objects per region, inventory; beyond the limit, the least recently used files are removed down to 85%
- ✅ 20 GB by default (adjustable up to 64 GB) + "Clear cache" button; size tracked as writes happen, the folder is only walked at startup, when the limit is exceeded and every 30 min
- ✅ Inventory cache (folders whose version has not changed restored without a request)
- ✅ Cache format like Firestorm: one raw file per asset, without encryption (Firestorm writes `sl_cache_<uuid>_0.asset` and keeps decoded sounds as WAV `.dsf`, `lldiskcache.cpp`, `llaudiodecodemgr.cpp`); encrypting would protect nothing (open source viewer, a copybot captures the data on the network, not in the cache); we keep our extensions (`.ogg`, `.mesh`…), a neutral extension like `.asset` would protect nothing either: content protection goes through the exports

## Controls

- ✅ Cursor after an Alt+click camera drag (human report, 2026-10-28; task `alt-click-cursor-return`; no issue; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-28): like LLToolCamera::handleMouseUp (lltoolfocus.cpp), a camera drag that began on a valid point (Alt / Ctrl+Alt / Ctrl+Shift+Alt click, focus tool) gives the cursor back at the centre of the window, where the camera turned the clicked point, so it is still over what was clicked; avatar steering and a focus tool click on the sky still give it back where it was pressed. It came back at the press position, over whatever the turned camera now showed there.
- ✅ AltGr + left drag orbits the camera ([#1308](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1308); task `altgr-orbit`; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-28): on a keyboard layout with AltGr, Windows types the key as Ctrl + Alt, so in the Legacy Viewer AltGr + click and drag is the Ctrl + Alt orbit (LLToolCamera::handleHover, lltoolfocus.cpp), AltGr + Shift the pan. winit hides both modifiers while AltGr is held, so Aurora did nothing. AltGr now counts as Ctrl + Alt for the mouse only (click, drag, the camera cursor over the world, the destination marker's priority), never for the keyboard shortcuts, and not in mouselook, where it stays a character key; no camera cursor while typing in a field (`app/alt_graph.rs`). Right Alt on a layout without AltGr is a plain Alt, as before, and now also shows the Alt camera cursor. Demo: `AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA=altgr,x,y`.
- ✅ Show / hide HUDs shortcut (human request, 2026-10-27; task `hud-toggle-shortcut`; no issue; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-27, `AURORA_DEMO_HUDS=1`): Alt+Shift+H shows or hides the HUD attachments, as the Legacy Viewer's View › Show HUD Attachments (menu_viewer.xml, shortcut="alt|shift|H"; LLViewShowHUDAttachments, llviewermenu.cpp); rebindable in Preferences › Shortcuts › HUDs ("Show / hide HUDs"). The shortcut and World › HUDs › Show HUDs share one path: a HUD locked by RLVa keeps them shown, hiding them ends a HUD drag or hold and the focus of HUD media.
- ✅ Quickjump ([#1122](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1122); task `quickjump`; validated by the human in demo and on the grid on 2026-10-26): jump and land without the wait for the pre-jump and landing animations (FSIgnoreFinishAnimation, off by default). Like LLAgent::getControlFlags, every AgentUpdate then carries FINISH_ANIM (`AgentState::quickjump_flags`), not only the one sent when an animation stops (`animation_stop_flags`, unchanged). Me › Quickjump is a check entry (no longer greyed) and Preferences › Mouse and keyboard › Keyboard has "Jump and land without the animation delay", both on the saved `quickjump` setting.
- ✅ Lighter Controls window (issue [#1035](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1035); task `camera-controls-compact`; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-26, merge authorized): remove the Alt / Ctrl+Alt / Ctrl+Shift+Alt drag and Escape key list from the Camera tab, which only describes moving the camera without the window (floater_camera.xml shows no such list); the Movement tab keeps its key hints. A window with a compact minimized body (`Floater::compact_when_minimized`, the Controls window) shows the Performance window's compact / full icons (`arrows-minimize` / `arrows-maximize`, "Compact view" / "Full view") instead of the collapse dash. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 4,607 passed, zero failures; formatting and Clippy clean. Native French before/after captures of the Camera tab and the compact view inspected; Movement tab unchanged. Demo `AURORA_DEMO_CONTROLS=camera|compact`.
- ✅ Toggle crouch camera control (issue #1017; PR #1025; task `crouch-camera-1017`): preserve camera orbit, pan and zoom while crouch is latched, following `LLAgent::moveUp` (`indra/newview/llagent.cpp`); retain the crouch network control and camera reset for ordinary movement. Three regressions cover held/released toggle input, both avatar and world focus, every movement axis, held crouch, jumping and flight; the camera regression fails before the fix and passes afterward. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 5,274 passed, zero failed, 30 ignored; scripts, formatting and Clippy clean. Offline scenarios: `AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA=crouch-alt,x,y|crouch-pan,x,y|crouch-zoom,x,y`. Default dev viewer built from code commit `09e462dd`; native before/after captures of all three gestures and ordinary orbit/pan/zoom inspected. At frame 690 the corrected camera keeps world focus with Down active, then returns to the avatar after walking at 700; ordinary gestures retain the same behavior without Down. All six corrected/native comparison runs exit zero with no errors. Validated by the human in the test viewer on 2026-10-25; merge explicitly authorized.
- ✅ Close-window keyboard ownership (issue #987; PR #1008; task `close-window-movement-987`): reserve logical Ctrl+W before native held movement and gesture routing, including repeated presses and releasing Ctrl before W. Preserve window/conversation closure and ordinary movement. Follow `LLViewerInput::scanKey` / `LLViewerWindow::handleKey`; share egui's logical-letter and non-Latin physical fallback. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 5,243 passed, zero failed, 30 ignored; scripts, formatting and Clippy clean. Regressions cover native ownership across layouts, repeats, modifier release order, fresh movement and neighbouring shortcut combinations, alongside existing floater/conversation closure tests. Native offline demo on code commit `4393df88`: Preferences closes once while the key repeats, no avatar/camera displacement (0.000 mm) during Ctrl+W or after releasing Ctrl first; a fresh ordinary arrow press moves the avatar normally and stops on release. Three clean native captures inspected, with no warnings/errors in the measured run. Validated by the human in the test viewer on 2026-10-25; merge explicitly authorized. No grid test is needed.
- ✅ Stop animations from detached temporary attachments (human report, 2026-10-25; task `temporary-attachment-animations`; PR [#959](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/pull/959)): follow `LLVOAvatarSelf::detachObject` and `LLViewerObject::markDead` to stop motions from the removed root and linked prims, independently of inventory membership. Regressions cover body/HUD and regular/temporary attachments, automatic detach and drop, linked sources, repeated packets, unrelated motions and region crossings. The attachment regression passes. Native offline `AURORA_DEMO_WORN=temporary-detach|temporary-drop` captures inspected; both logs confirm a child-prim dance active before detach, one outgoing stop and the dance inactive afterward, with no warnings or errors. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 5,170 passed, zero failures, 30 ignored; scripts, formatting and Clippy clean. Default dev viewer built. Validated by the human in the test viewer on 2026-10-25; merge authorized.
- ✅ Avatar Go to and Follow ([#745](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/745); task `avatar-follow`), validated on the grid by the human on 2026-10-24: translated avatar/attachment and resident-list actions; fixed Go to destination, persistent follow, 2 m arrival and 3 m restart hysteresis, precise seated-target transforms and region-origin shifts. Received locomotion signals drive takeoff and nearby landing on our own support, respecting region/parcel/RLVa flight policy. Go to accepts late flight signals; ground follow temporarily runs with a running leader and restores the user preference. Flight uses one continuous burn per axis toward a nearby point at target height, then retains the release without final approach impulses. Manual movement, Escape, Stop following, teleport or target loss cancels travel; direct travel does not navigate around walls. Full scripts/check.ps1 green: 4,952 passed, zero failures, 29 ignored; formatting and Clippy clean. Native captures cover EN/FR menus, Go to/follow, cancellation, running restoration, delayed flight with prediction on/off and two takeoff/landing cycles. At 300 ms ping plus 40 ms jitter, arrival rests at 2.297 m / 2.377 m with 0.022 m / 0.023 m height error, zero speed and STOP over the final 15 seconds. Demo: `AURORA_DEMO_AVATAR_TRAVEL=<mode>` and opt-in `AURORA_DEMO_LINK=0,native-flight` (full-speed flight/inertia, ordinary ground fixtures unchanged).
- ✅ Sit-on-ground shortcut and separate sitting hover heights (human request, 2026-10-20; task `seated-hover-height`): Alt+Shift+S sits on the ground, or stands up when sitting, as the Legacy Viewer's Movement › Sit Down / Stand Up (menu_viewer.xml, shortcut="alt|shift|S"; enable_sitdown_self: no ground sit while flying); rebindable in Preferences › Controls ("Sit on the ground / stand up"). Aurora addition: the Hover Height window gets Standing / Sitting on an object / Sitting on the ground tabs; each sitting posture can have its own height (off by default: the standing height, manual or automatic, applies). The window follows the current posture; the height of the posture is drawn at once and sent again through AgentPreferences whenever the avatar sits down or stands up. Saved per account in `hover_height.json` (older files holding the standing height alone still load). Demo: `AURORA_DEMO_HOVER_HEIGHT=sit-ground`. The human validated the shortcut, both sitting heights, what other viewers see and persistence on the grid on 2026-10-20.
- ✅ Camera limits off by default (human choice of 2026-10-16, a difference from the Legacy Viewer, whose DisableCameraConstraints and FSIgnoreSimulatorCameraConstraints are off): Preferences › Camera › Limits, "Unrestricted camera" and "Walk through objects", renamed "Disable camera collisions", are both on by default; settings version 14 turns them on once in earlier files (they all wrote the old "off"), a later choice stays. Validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-16
- ✅ HUD movement default ([#266](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/266)): Ctrl replaces Alt for direct dragging and local wheel resizing. Settings version 10 migrates the old Alt-only default once; other saved bindings, secondary keys and cleared slots are preserved, and an Alt choice made afterwards survives reload. HUD demos and documentation updated. `check.ps1` green: 2,018 passed, 24 ignored, formatting and Clippy clean; the sandbox's denied audio loopback socket passed with local network access allowed. Demo captures inspected: Ctrl drag and resize, Ctrl secondary binding, Ctrl shown in shortcut preferences, and camera orbit crossing a HUD without moving it. This direct HUD gesture is an Aurora feature; Firestorm's Alt camera behavior is retained. Validated by the human in the online test "Test hud-drag-ctrl-online" on 2026-10-14; merge authorized.
- ✅ Letter shortcuts follow the keyboard layout (`fix/letter-shortcuts-layout`, validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-14 on an AZERTY keyboard: M, Ctrl+M, Ctrl+Shift+M, Ctrl+Shift+A, Ctrl+ù, the Preferences display and ZQSD; local keyboard handling, nothing to check on the grid): the mnemonic default shortcuts (M, Ctrl+M, Ctrl+Shift+M, Ctrl+Shift+A, Ctrl+`…) were bound by QWERTY position, so on AZERTY M was the "," key. They now follow the key Windows names that way in the active layout, like Firestorm's virtual-key table (LLKeyboardWin32::LLKeyboardWin32): `keybinds/layout.rs` reads each key's virtual key (`key_for`, `named_key`) and `KeyBindings::for_layout` moves every default shortcut except walking, strafing, jump and crouch, which keep their places under the hand (WASD / ZQSD, E, C). On French and Belgian AZERTY: M, Ctrl+M, Ctrl+Shift+M on the M key, Ctrl+Shift+A on the A key, Ctrl+` on ù (VK_OEM_3, as in Firestorm, not ²); on German QWERTZ, Ctrl+` is ö and the other defaults stay (none uses Y or Z). Settings version 12 moves a binding still on its former default once; a key chosen by the user, a cleared slot, or a default whose new key another action already uses stays. The Preferences already showed the bound key's own character (M, Ù); the keycap now lowers a capital's accent into the cap (`ui/widgets/keycap.rs`: Ù was drawn as U). Demo `AURORA_DEMO_KEYBOARD=zqsd|qwertz` simulates the layouts; unit tests for US QWERTY, French and Belgian AZERTY and German QWERTZ.
- ✅ Right-click tolerance (2026-10-13), validated by the human in "Test right-click-tolerance", merge authorised: small motion and back-and-forth jitter remain a context-menu click, while a deliberate held drag still orbits. Replace the accumulated 4-unit travel threshold with 10 UI points of net displacement and latch orbit until release. Deliberate Aurora extension to Firestorm's LLToolPie::handleRightMouseDown (which opens the menu on press); no hold-time requirement. Four unit regressions cover diagonal motion, repeated jitter, slow motion / return and interface scale. `check.ps1` green with sequential tests: 1,982 passed, 24 ignored, formatting and Clippy clean (two existing render-thread timing tests failed in parallel and passed sequentially; the sandbox initially denied an audio loopback socket). Demo `AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA=right-click|right-jitter|right-drag|right-slow,x,y`: screenshots and logs verify a stationary camera and menu for small motion / jitter, orbit without a menu for fast / slow drags; pie menu, left steering and mouselook rechecked, no GPU warnings or errors.
- ✅ Object contents: "Copy to inventory" and "Copy and wear" (#1103, task `contents-copy-inventory`; validated by the human on the grid on 2026-10-26). The Open window (LLFloaterOpenObject, floater_openobject.xml) gets the Legacy Viewer's copy button and its two wear entries ("Replace outfit with contents", "Add contents to outfit"), greyed with the reason while the listing loads, the object is gone or an item is refused. Rules of move_inv_category_world_to_agent: every item copyable and transferable, or the object ours and the no-copy items moved, after the MoveInventoryFromObject / MoveInventoryFromScriptedObject warning (the existing "Removing object contents" preferences, with its "do not show again"); one refused item refuses the whole copy. A new folder named after the object (under the root, under Clothing for a wear), one MoveTaskInventory per item, the folder selected in the inventory (callbackMoveInventory); a wear waits for every item to arrive like LLInventoryCopyAndWearObserver, then wears the folder (60 s at most, then an alert and the outfit unchanged). The editing motion is local and driven by ViewerEffect type 15, not an AgentAnimation request. The arm follows with a 0.1 s half-life and releases in 0.5 s; gun aiming adds a constrained torso correction and suppresses body noise. Editing and targeting use joint priority 2, with activation order breaking ties; the editing hand request has priority 3. Attachments and control avatars are excluded and edit pauses retain the pose. Native scenarios: `AURORA_DEMO_POINTING=1|release|private|remote|remote-release|aim`.

Position, rotation and temporary stop on by default; size and contents off. Progress by step and copied item; the other link numbers and placements preserved, up to 256 prims, ObjectLink order kept after confirmation of the parenting, without depending on the order of the packets. Copy through the resident's inventory, rights and name conflicts checked before unlinking, restoration of the initial states of the scripts, recovery of partial copies and lost replies, old prim placed at the initial position of the replacement (positions swapped when their copy is enabled). The copied scripts start in their initial state; Linkset Data stays in the old root when it is replaced. Twenty-four targeted tests, `check.ps1` green after integrating main and PR #149 (1,182 tests, 15 ignored), button / options / progress / result screenshots and `AURORA_DEMO_REPLACE_PRIM` scenario. Validated by the human on 2026-10-12 in the online test viewer, after the copy of the script and the physics properties fix. The review also covers a script named Contents: the virtual category of the contents is not a name conflict and the type of the inserted item is checked.
- ✅ CTRL + wheel in Edit mode: looping walk through the objects really under the pointer, sorted by depth, with a single entry per linkset; Linked edit: only the targeted prims of the same object as the main selection. Rays against the triangles through the picking index, tests in parallel, no GPU wait; gesture takes priority over media and camera / HUD zooms, wheel fractions accumulated, selection replaced with ObjectSelect / ObjectDeselect. Requested difference: Firestorm's LLViewerWindow::handleScrollWheel only zooms; units and selection messages kept like LLToolSelect::handleObjectSelection. Demo `AURORA_DEMO_BUILD=cycle,object:99610` / `cycle-linked,object:99610`: A → B → C → B and root A → part A2 → root A → part A2, camera / HUD zoom unchanged; before / after screenshots examined, no GPU validation problem. `check.ps1` green (1085 tests passed, 13 ignored), including 7 new tests (real geometry, depth, reverse direction, loop, restriction to the linkset, multiple selection, high-precision wheel). Validated in demo then online by the human on 2026-10-12 in "Test selection-ctrl-molette" and "Test selection-ctrl-molette-en-ligne": walk through the objects and through the prims of the same object in Linked edit. Feature merged by PR #143.
- ✅ Object rez by drag and drop from the inventory: rules checked in LLToolDragAndDrop and FSCommon::getGroupForRezzing, placement on terrain / object, Ctrl towards the contents with modify / transfer permissions, single consumption of no-copy items and restoration by the server on refusal. Attachments and Marketplace listings refused; library, trash, links and neighbouring regions taken into account. "Rez under the land group" option on by default, active group as fallback. Implementation specific to Aurora: selection index reused, alpha masks prepared by the decoding tasks, 100 ms cursor cache and new computation on release, no GPU wait. Measured on 16,002 objects: 54.5 µs against 1,917.3 µs for the full walk, 2 candidates and same target. `AURORA_DEMO_REZ=1|land|object|contents|nocopy|cancel`; before / after screenshots examined, a single command per drop, zero for Esc, inventory / linked prims / animesh verified. `check.ps1` green (936 tests passed, 13 ignored). Validated by the human on 2026-10-11 in "Test rez-inventaire", with agreement for the merge.
- ✅ Sitting on an object like Firestorm: root placed at the seat's offset, without the size / hover correction of the figure, reserved for the ground; hover of the appearance kept in the avatar's frame (tilted seat). AvatarAnimation sources kept per animation until it stops; on a change of parent, AgentAnimation STOP requests for the seat and its elements, without stopping the AOs of the other objects. References: LLVOAvatar::updateRootPositionAndRotation / getOffObject, process_avatar_animation, LLVOAvatarSelf::stopMotionFromSource. Demo AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA=sit: animation started by a linked prim at frame 300, stop requested on standing up at 700; before / after screenshots seated and standing compared. Seven tests added (seated / standing cache, tilted hover, sources / sequences, AO, stop / resume, change of seat, round trip with the demo simulator); check.ps1 green (533 passed, 7 ignored). Validated by the human on the grid on 2026-10-09 in "Test assise-animations": position in the armchair and animations stopping on standing up.
- ✅ Jump stuck in pre-jump: single notification to the simulator of the ends of our avatar's non-looping animations (LLMotionController::updateMotionsByType / LLVOAvatarSelf::requestStopMotion / LLAgent::requestStopMotion / onAnimStop): AgentAnimation stop and FINISH_ANIM after a brief press; landings protected for 1 s after the last jump command (Firestorm's default RecentJumpThresholdSecs), pre-jump never removed by this protection, getting up covered too. Our avatar stays evaluated even far from the camera. 5 tests added (late loading, start of the fade, single notification, key held, jumps close together, loops and server stop); `check.ps1` green (469 tests passed, 4 ignored; local HTTP servers of the tests allowed), standard and loop demos captured and examined with no rendering error. Fix validated by the human on the grid on 2026-10-09 in "Test saut-pre-jump", after a request to test a brief Page↑, several successive jumps and Page↑ held to fly.
- ✅ Left click on linked prims: identification by the triangles of the prim on hover and on click, even if the boxes of the root table and of the chairs overlap; Touch on the table and Sit on the chair, with the sit request targeting the child. Reference: LLViewerWindow::cursorIntersect / LLVOVolume::lineSegmentIntersect and LLToolPie::final_click_action. Two tests added (overlapping boxes, real bounds of prim / sculpt / mesh, invisible faces and IGNORE); defect reproduced with the old selection. `check.ps1` green (439 tests passed, 4 ignored). Demo `AURORA_DEMO_ACTIONS=linked-touch|linked-sit`: Touch / Sit cursors and actions on the right prims confirmed in the logs; screenshots examined, Sit / Touch / Buy / Ignore / Disabled scenarios verified. Validated by the human on 2026-10-09 on the table and its chairs on the grid in "Test clic-assise-prims-liees".
- ✅ Movement of our avatar like Firestorm: LLViewerObject::interpolateLinearMotion (velocity / acceleration of the full and terse updates, 1/45 s physics step, time dilation of the simulator, extrapolation kept as long as the main circuit responds; slowdown after 2 s and stop after 3 s of silence), LLDrawable::updateXform / LLSmoothInterpolation (half-life 0.06 s, correction thresholds), terrain / known region limits, state reset on arrival / on standing back up, position drawn in the right frame when crossing regions. No simulation of the controls on the grid; local physics reserved for the demo. 12 non-regression tests added; `check.ps1` green (240 tests passed, 4 ignored), walking demo captured with GPU validation without error. Movement tested and validated by the human on Agni on 2026-10-08 ("Test prediction-avatar" viewer).

Main arrows and saved shortcuts kept, including cleared keys; restoring the default values also applies this detection. Deliberate difference from Firestorm's fixed WASD letters (`app_settings/key_bindings.xml`, `llkeyboardwin32.cpp`): adaptation to the active layout. Demo `AURORA_DEMO_KEYBOARD=system|wasd|zqsd|fallback`, 7 tests added, `check.ps1` green (286 passed, 4 ignored), screenshots verified. Validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-09: ZQSD selected automatically and movement working after closing the preferences.
- ✅ Movement like Firestorm: keyboard rotation at 90°/s with a ramp-up over 0.25 s (getYawRate); FAST_AT / FAST_LEFT / FAST_UP as soon as the key is held (running goes through SetAlwaysRun); displayed body separate from the agent's frame (LLVOAvatar::updateOrientation, for us and the others): at rest it keeps its orientation up to ~45° of difference then turns (TURN_LEFT / TURN_RIGHT sent to the simulator for the turning animations), while walking it faces the direction of movement (sidesteps = sideways, walking backwards = it turns round towards the camera, Firestorm's default setting), lag 0.4 s on the ground / 0.22 s in the air / 0.15 s in mouselook, no rotation when sitting on the ground or getting up; after a camera orbit, a movement key turns the agent towards the camera (FSResetCameraOnMovement). Demo: AURORA_DEMO_KEY=down|up|left|right
- ✅ Left click held on the avatar: the avatar follows the camera; arrows relative to the camera (→ = turn right and walk, double press = run); + right click = move forward, double right click held = run (same double press as the Forward key, agent_handle_doubletap_run; releasing either of the two buttons stops the run; demo: AURORA_DEMO_MMO="x,y,2"; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-09); right click dragged = orbit; wheel = zoom
- ✅ Left click held on our name tag = as on the avatar (mouse steering, Alt+click aimed at us): Firestorm takes the name tags in the pick (LLPipeline::lineSegmentIntersectInWorld, after the world: an object in front of the name tag keeps the click). Demo: AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA=tag. Validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-09
- ✅ Right click on the name tag of an avatar (one's own included) = its right-click menu, as on the avatar: same pick as Firestorm (LLPipeline::lineSegmentIntersectInWorld: an object in front of the name tag keeps the click, the closest name tag wins); "Zoom" frames the avatar. Demo: AURORA_DEMO_RCLICK=tag. Validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-09
- ✅ Labels under the interface (validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-13; GPU cost measured: +0.017 ms on `g_post`, 5 alternated passes): the name plates, the classic name tags, the floating texts of objects and the destination beacon no longer pass in front of a worn HUD, a window, the top bar, the bottom bar or the chat messages. They are painted before the whole interface and drawn by the renderer before the worn HUDs (`aurora_render::end_world_labels`, `ui_world.rs`); the floating text of a HUD stays on its HUD, under the bars. The glass of the name plates blurs the scene alone, where they are (blur limited to their area); that of the windows and bars blurs the scene with its labels and the HUDs. Deliberate difference from Firestorm, requested by the human: Firestorm draws the labels over the worn HUDs (`render_ui` in `llviewerdisplay.cpp`: `render_hud_attachments`, then `LLHUDObject::renderAll`); the beacon is already under the HUDs there (`render_hud_elements`). Demo: `AURORA_DEMO_HUDS=1` with `AURORA_DEMO_TAGS=voice`; under the top bar: `AURORA_DEMO_TAGS=voice` with `AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA=close,400,420,54`.
- ✅ Demo mode: movement simulated locally (walking, running, flying, jumping, ground / plaza) to test the controls without a grid
- ✅ Ctrl+Alt+click = aim at a point and orbit around it; Esc / click on the avatar = return
- ✅ Click on one's avatar to steer the view, even when not completely loaded: cursor ray against the avatar's box (size given by the simulator, rotated with the body); surface hit inside the box = avatar, and if the ray crosses the box without hitting anything (body not drawn yet), a click less than 20 cm from the axis of the body = avatar (before: depth point in a fixed capsule, missed when the body was not drawn)
- ✅ Alt+click (Ctrl+Alt to orbit) on our own avatar: aims at the clicked point and orbits around it (inspect a part of the avatar), like Firestorm; return with Esc, a simple click on the avatar or by moving
- ✅ Sit / Buy / Pay cursors and left click like Firestorm (LLToolPie, LLFloaterBuy / Pay): original native Windows 32 × 32 cursors, hotspot (20, 15), same Buy / Pay drawing; action of a child or inheritance from the root, attachments excluded, Sit only when standing, Buy only when for sale, Pay only if the object or its root accepts money. Sit sends AgentRequestSit to the object's region; purchase (original / copy / contents) and payment wait for a confirmation, the proposed price and a sufficient known balance; ObjectBuy uses the root and the Objects folder, MoneyTransferRequest targets the clicked prim. `llSetPayPrice`: free amount, four buttons at most, field hidden at −1. The re-read properties prevent a confirmation if the offer or the owner has changed. The cursors disappear over the interface, in build mode, with the modifiers or in mouselook. Offline demo `AURORA_DEMO_ACTIONS=1|sit|buy|pay|buy-confirm|pay-confirm`, simulated purchases / payments; tests and screenshots verified, operation confirmed by the human locally then online in "Test curseurs-sit-buy-pay" on 2026-10-09. Phosphor exception requested: Firestorm's drawings reused.
- ✅ Click actions 0–9 completed like Firestorm: NONE = TOUCH (press, hold / move, release and surface information), physical Grab; DISABLED removes Touch and the inheritance without by itself locking the physical grab; IGNORE excludes only the geometry of the prim concerned outside build mode and lets what is behind be reached. Open shows the contents of the root if not empty and owner / modifiable (RequestTaskInventory capability, otherwise UDP transfer bounded in memory); Play toggles play / pause of the parcel media; Open Media toggles this playback if the face already shows a media texture, otherwise opens the parcel URL; Zoom frames the object's box and follows it. Firestorm drawings and hotspots for all these cursors. Interactive transparent faces taken into account, panels invisible to the click; purchase on a child checked again from this prim even when the transaction is on the root. `AURORA_DEMO_ACTIONS` demo extended, offline tests / screenshots and loading of the local page through CEF verified; pause states, external opening and transactions simulated. All click actions confirmed by the human locally then in real conditions in the connected viewer on 2026-10-09.
- ✅ Payment and purchase windows and llDialog menus aligned with Firestorm with the Aurora style: equal quick amounts in two columns, free field then Pay / Cancel; seller, object, transferable contents and restrictions of the next owner before Buy / Cancel; titles according to original / copy / contents. The sale of the contents requires at least one item copiable by the seller and transferable to the buyer, delivered to the root of the inventory. llDialog: three equal columns, rows from bottom to top without changing the indices or the labels sent to the script, empty spaces kept, long text truncated with a tooltip, Block / Ignore below; the menu stays displayed until the reply, Ignore does not reply to the script, Block uses the object block list. Demo: `AURORA_DEMO_ACTIONS=pay-layout|pay-hidden|pay-large|buy-original|buy-contents|buy-empty`, `AURORA_DEMO_DIALOG=12|4|long`. Formatting, clippy, 441 tests and offline screenshots verified; payment and purchase windows and llDialog menus validated online by the human in "Test fenetre-paiement" on 2026-10-09.

Choice immediate and saved in Preferences › Interface › Layout › Right-click menu. Reference: Firestorm's `PieMenu::show / handleHover / handleMouseButtonUp` and `menu_pie_*.xml`. Deliberate differences: list kept by default for existing users (UsePieMenu is enabled in Firestorm), diameter adapted to the Phosphor icons and to the labels, system pointer not moved at the edges, Back button and right click to go up at the human's request (Firestorm selects with both buttons), Aurora's extra actions reachable through "More"; Zoom takes the "Go here" direction of the terrain as long as the autopilot is missing, Show in inventory takes that of the gestures on attachments. Demo: `AURORA_DEMO_PIE=1|0` with `AURORA_DEMO_RCLICK` or `AURORA_DEMO_HUDS=menu`, saved preference unchanged. check.ps1 green (933 tests passed, 12 ignored, sequential tests for the render timings), list / avatars / terrain at the edge / object / submenus / HUD / preferences screenshots examined. Validated by the human on 2026-10-11 in demo: menu, actions, submenus and preferences toggle, then transparency and going back a single level by right click.
- ✅ Camera like Firestorm (`camera/` module, port of LLAgentCamera, LLToolCamera, LLAgent::pitch) — validated on Agni on 2026-10-09, `AURORA_DEMO_CAMERA` demo scenarios: rear view by default (CameraOffsetRearView −3 / 0 / 0.75 above the head, FocusOffsetRearView 1 / 0 / 1), rear / front / side / shoulder presets (app_settings/camera) or custom; smoothing in the avatar's space (CameraPositionSmoothing × 0.02 s), smoothed distance (0.07 s), pull-back in the air according to speed (DynamicCameraStrength, after 0.5 s in the air, 5 m at most, avatar always in front), animated transitions between the views (ZoomTime 0.4 s, smoothstep, stop at 0.3 m from the head towards mouselook, leaving mouselook by zooming out from the head), collision plane of the simulator (CameraConstraint), never less than 0.5 m from the ground nor beyond the draw distance; wheel: zoom by the fourth root of 2 (×0.25 to ×8), all the way into the head = mouselook, towards oneself in mouselook = exit (FSScrollWheelExitsMouselook), Ctrl / Shift + wheel raise the camera / the focus point; Alt camera (LLToolCamera): Alt+click aims at the point (at the middle of the object, pushed towards the surface up close, calcFocusOffset; an attachment aims at its avatar), Alt+drag zooms and turns, Ctrl+Alt+drag orbits, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+drag pans, 4 px before moving, one full turn per window width; too close to a prim the field narrows (calcCameraMinDistance); the focused object is followed when it moves (TrackFocusObject); click on one's avatar: return behind it (except ClickOnAvatarKeepsCamera) then mouse steering; moving brings the camera back behind the avatar, turned towards where it was looking (FSResetCameraOnMovement), Esc too without turning the avatar (ResetViewTurnsAvatar); seat camera (AvatarSitResponse: llSetCameraEyeOffset / AtOffset, llForceMouselook), agent frame that turns with the seat, no head offset when seated; return to the avatar on arrival from a teleport (FSResetCameraOnTP); Firestorm's keys (Alt+← / → orbit, Alt+↑ / ↓ move closer / further away, Alt+Page Up / Down and Ctrl+Alt+↑ / ↓ go above / below, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+arrows pan; the most specific combination wins, Alt+← no longer turns the avatar). Preferences › Camera: preset and offsets, field of view, distance, duration of the transitions, smoothing, pull-back in flight, return when moving / after a teleport, Esc turns the avatar, click on the avatar keeps the camera, follow the focused object, hover height, wheel without zoom, camera without limits, pass through objects, mouselook and exit with the wheel. Deliberate difference: the dragged right click and the arrows with the left click held turn the camera around the avatar without turning it (Firestorm turns the agent)
- ✅ Look-at tracking (LookAt), like Firestorm: head / neck / torso (LLHeadRotMotion: 35% to the torso, the rest half neck half head, 72° at most, lags 0.15 / 0.27 s) and eyes (LLEyeMotion: 27° at most, convergence, small movements and random glances away) in the animation blend (medium priority, animations of higher priority keep the head); target of our avatar like LLAgentCamera::updateLookAt (straight ahead at rest, cursor ±45° when the mouse moves, mouselook, point aimed at by the camera with Ctrl+Alt+click = FOCUS, avatar that spoke before in the chat one time out of two = AUTO_LISTEN), priorities and delays from attentions.xml; sent to the others by ViewerEffect (type 14, 57 bytes) and look-at received from the other avatars; options Controls → Look-at: "Look-at tracking" disabled by default (nothing sent, head straight) and "Look-at range" 1–64 m (64 = unlimited, target brought back to that distance from the head: FSLookAtTargetMaxDistance); when it is disabled, a "none" LookAt is sent so that the others stop displaying the old one. Demo: AURORA_DEMO_LOOKAT=1

## Interface

- ✅ New logo (human request, 2026-10-29; task `new-logo`; no issue; PR [#1398](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/pull/1398); validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-29): the howling wolf with aurora ribbons replaces the geometric wolf everywhere. `assets/branding/` holds the new set (`aurora-logo` in colour, white, black, violet, teal and on navy; SVG + PNG) and `aurora.ico` rebuilt from it; the viewer reads `aurora-logo.png` (window icon, login, loading, About, system notifications) and `aurora-logo-white.svg` (Aurora menu button) from there, without a copy in the crate. The system notification icon's cache file is named after the logo, so existing installs show the new one. The wolf of the Aurora menu button is rasterised at 40 px instead of 128 (sampled down without mipmaps, its thin ribbons broke into dots at 18 px). README, BRANDING and the README's login screenshot (logo swapped in the existing capture) updated; the website follows in its own repository. Demo captures checked: login (`AURORA_DEMO_LOGIN=cards2`), loading, toolbar, About (`AURORA_DEMO_ABOUT=info`). ⬜ Outside the repository, for the human: GitHub organisation avatar, repositories' social preview, Discord server icon.
- ✅ Inventory hover defaults to Add (issue #1344; PR #1377; task `inventory-hover-add`, 2026-10-29; validated by the human in the online test viewer on 2026-10-29): unworn objects and stackable clothing offer an explicit Add action that preserves worn items regardless of double-click preferences. Links act on their original; Library items copy into inventory before adding. Worn items retain Take off; body parts and physics retain Wear because they cannot stack. This requested Aurora hover default uses the Legacy Viewer's explicit additive operation (`LLObjectBridge::performAction("wear_add")`, `indra/newview/llinventorybridge.cpp`), while double-click and details actions keep their existing defaults. Appearance actions wait while an inventory mutation or appearance operation is pending. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 6,068 passed, zero failures, 30 ignored; scripts, formatting and Clippy clean. Native pointer tests exercise all three widths, both double-click preferences, links, Library copies and a busy appearance. French demo captures confirm Add changes to Take off after clicking, with Current Outfit growing from 13 to 14 links.
- ✅ Window layout saved as soon as it changes ([#1319](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1319); [PR #1359](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/pull/1359); task `layout-autosave`, 2026-10-28; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-28 (windows moved, resized and collapsed, viewer ended from the Task Manager, layout back at the next start), no grid test needed): `ui_layout.ron` was written at exit only, so a crash or a forced close lost the whole session's arrangement, and a failed write left no trace. The layout is now also saved two seconds after it stops changing (a click released over the interface, the viewer window resized), serialized and written on a background thread (`ui::layout::Autosave`); numbered snapshots keep a late background save from replacing the exit's or bringing back a reset layout, and every failure is logged (`ui layout not saved: …`). The Legacy Viewer writes its floater rects at exit only (LLFloater::storeRectControl, LLAppViewer::cleanup): a clean exit gives the same file. Measured on a real 400 KB layout (dev build): 0.87 ms on the main thread (anchors and clone), once per burst of changes, and 2.0 ms of serialization on the background thread. Unit tests `the_autosave_waits_for_the_layout_to_settle`, `an_older_layout_never_replaces_a_newer_one`; full `scripts/check.ps1` green (6,057 passed, 30 ignored). The reporter was asked how they quit and what was reset, in case the reset has another cause.
- ✅ Default permissions from the inventory New menu (human request, 2026-10-28; [PR #1367](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/pull/1367); task `inventory-default-permissions`): add a final entry opening saved defaults for scripts, notecards, gestures, materials, wearables and environment settings, plus the same per-type import defaults as the unchanged import Permissions tab. Apply creation rights to inventory items, wearable assets and new object scripts; keep existing items and Save As rights. Follow `LLFloaterPermsDefault`, `create_new_item`, `LLAgentWearables::createWearable` and creation callbacks. Demos: `AURORA_DEMO_INVENTORY=default-permissions|aurora-default-permissions`. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 6,070 passed, zero failed, 30 ignored; formatting, scripts and Clippy clean. Regressions cover migration, category isolation, mandatory transfer without copy, captured upload defaults, source permissions, wearable asset masks, callback identity/ownership and egui menu/import-grid pointer events. Default dev viewer built; English, French and Japanese captures inspected, alongside the existing French import Permissions tab. Validated by the human in the online test viewer on 2026-10-28; merge authorized.
- ✅ Add local files from the texture picker ([#1341](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1341); task `picker-local-add`, 2026-10-28; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-28, no grid test needed: local files never leave the viewer): the picker's Local tab had only the importer's local previews and no way to add one. It now has the Legacy Viewer's Add / Remove / Upload buttons (LLFloaterTexturePicker::onBtnAdd, onBtnRemove, onBtnUpload, indra/newview/lltexturectrl.cpp): Add chooses images (or the glTF materials of a material picker) and keeps them as the importer's local previews, refreshed when the file changes; Remove drops the chosen one; Upload opens the importer on its type with the file loaded. Remove and Upload wait for a local file to be chosen. Unit test `local_tab_buttons_add_remove_and_upload_local_files`.
- ✅ Startup switches of Conversations and the mini-map ([#1320](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1320); task `startup-window-toggles`, 2026-10-28; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-28, no grid test needed): Preferences › Layout › Startup could not turn off "Conversations at startup" nor "Mini-map at startup" while the window was open, the window's state overwriting the setting at every frame. The window's state at exit is still remembered (save_visibility of floater_im_container.xml and floater_map.xml), but only when the window opens or closes (`ui::StartupWindows`): in between, the switch holds.
- ✅ ":" as a synonym for "/me" ([#1202](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1202); task `chat-colon-emote`, 2026-10-27; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-27): Preferences › Chat › `colon_emote`, off like the Legacy Viewer's AllowMUpose. A line that starts with ":" leaves as an emote on channel 0 and in the conversations (private, group, conference): ":waves" is "/me waves", ":'s here" "/me's here"; a line of three bytes or less, or a colon followed by a digit, a punctuation mark or a space (":)", ":3"), stays as it is (FSCommon::applyMuPose, before the gestures). With the option, a line starting with ":" shows us typing only with "Also when emoting" (FSNearbyChat::handleChatBarKeystroke).
- ✅ Expanded inventory layout (issue #1020; PR #1073; task `inventory-columns-1020`, 2026-10-26): saved sidebar and adjacent-column resizing, optional Type/Permissions/Modified columns, mandatory Name and layout reset. Medium views give permissions their own resizable column; compact trees retain inline CMT. Smaller windows fit without overwriting the requested widths, and settings save after pointer release. Names, tags, rename fields and quick actions remain inside Name with full control height. Four regressions cover actual splitters at 100%/150%, stationary dragging, scrollbar/header alignment and ordinary selection; column-menu input; every visibility/rights combination; old/partial settings and persistence. Full post-rebase `scripts/check.ps1` green: 5,478 passed, zero failed, 30 ignored; scripts, formatting and Clippy clean. Default dev executable built without compiler warnings. Native French custom-width, menu/toggle/reset, compact, 150% and hovered-action captures inspected, retaining the 60 FPS cap with no logged errors. Demo: `aurora-columns` / `aurora-columns-hidden`. Validated by the human in the offline demo on 2026-10-26; merge explicitly authorized.
- ✅ Appearance outfit multiselection (#994; PR #1061; task `appearance-multiselect`; code `cec0c6147`): Ctrl/Cmd-click toggles outfits and Shift-click selects ranges in the filtered/sorted order, shared by gallery and list. Right-click preserves the selected group; Wear, Add and Take off use one combined COF change, deduplicate shared originals and preserve required body parts. Conflicting body parts follow the last selected outfit in display order (`LLAppearanceMgr::filterWearableItems`). Combined replacement has no single saved base; Save as saves the combination. Only the focused outfit opens by default, and selection counts/readiness are cached until selection or inventory changes. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 5,403 passed, zero failed, 30 ignored; 11 new regressions, including real widget clicks, distinct contributions from both outfits, loading failures and attachment limits. Eight native offline captures inspected in French/English, including Add (keeps the base, six attachments), Wear (ten combined items, three attachments) and the group context menu; no logged errors. Validated by the human in the test viewer on 2026-10-26; merge explicitly authorized. Post-rebase checks: 5,429 passed, zero failed, 30 ignored; formatting, scripts and Clippy clean.
- ✅ Optional outfit preview fill (issue #1012; PR #1055; task `outfit-preview-fill`): saved gallery option to fill preview frames with a centred UV crop, preserving image proportions. Whole-image Fit remains the default. - ✅ Minimized windows ([#269](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/269), [#276](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/276)): the toolbar button, the menu entry and the shortcut of a minimized window expand it and bring it to the front instead of closing it; a window shown closes, a closed one opens expanded, also when it was closed minimized (`LLFloaterReg::toggleInstance`, `LLFloater::openFloater` / `closeFloater` unminimize; `widgets::toggle_floater`, `open_floater`). The minimized bar is 200 points wide at most instead of the window's width (Firestorm `UIMinimizedWidth`, 160, with a smaller title font). While messages are unread, the minimized Conversations bar lights up with an accent outline and the toolbar button keeps its dot (Firestorm flashes the button while the conversations are closed or minimized, `LLIMMgr`, `flashCommand`). Deliberate differences from Firestorm: the outline also counts unread nearby chat, like the existing toolbar dot (Firestorm flashes for instant messages only), and a minimized bar stays where the window stood, shortened from its left edge (Firestorm moves it to the bottom-left row). Demo `AURORA_DEMO_MINIMIZED=1|expand`. `check.ps1` green (2,020 passed, 24 ignored). Validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-14.
- ✅ Stable name tag height for tall avatars ([#261](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/261)): the upright root-to-skull chain (including Bento spine joints), shape scales, mesh joint offsets and animation translation envelopes replace the fixed 1.05 m offset. The envelope is computed once per loaded animation track and blended with the same position priorities; held deformations and reset animations follow the rendered skeleton. Deliberate difference from Firestorm's `LLVOAvatar::idleCalcNameTagPosition`, requested by the human: the tag stays centred above the root, with 0.2 m clearance, without following posed head/body rotations or animation bobbing. A change of animation can change its envelope. Plate and classic styles and click depth share the anchor.
  - Verified in demo on 2026-10-13: `AURORA_DEMO_TAGS=height` (Nova and Lune enlarged by a translation deformer, animated head pitch and pelvis bobbing), captures at frames 660 / 780; classic tags; `wall` occlusion. Before: Nova's face behind its plate; after: plate above it and stable across the animation. No errors in the three corrected demo logs. At the 60 fps cap, the height scene stays at 60 fps / 16.67 ms before and after (11 posed avatars; shared machine and differing open panels, not a performance gain measurement).
  - `check.ps1` green: 1,987 passed, 24 ignored; format and Clippy pass. Tests cover animated deformation without head tracking, shape and mesh offsets, Bento spine, look-at, position priority and reset. The sandbox blocked a localhost audio test (10013); the complete suite passed with local socket access. The human validated the height demo and authorized the merge on 2026-10-13.
  - Validated online by the human on 2026-10-14 with build `0.4.0+7.g4972f989d` after the merge of #265: the name tag clears the deformed avatar and stays stable without following its head. Grid validation is complete.
- ✅ **Dyslexia-friendly font** ([#851](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/851), 2026-10-24; task `dyslexia-font`; validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-24): OpenDyslexic (Regular and Bold 0.910.12, SIL OFL 1.1, unmodified, about 450 KB) embedded beside Inter, Noto Sans and Roboto as a fourth choice of Preferences › Appearance › Bundled fonts, its Bold for the semi-bold texts; Inter stays the default. Emoji, CJK and symbols keep coming from the fallback fonts. The bundled fonts row now takes both columns of the page (four names; OpenDyslexic draws wide). Menu rows round their height up to a whole point: with the tall line of OpenDyslexic it was fractional and the letters stood 0.6 px off the row's middle at 1.25 (`labels_are_centred_on_their_icons_with_every_font` now checks OpenDyslexic too). Demo: Preferences › Appearance, the inventory, conversations and nearby chat in French and English with `AURORA_UI_FONT=OpenDyslexic` (accents, ligatures Æ Œ, colour emoji in the conversations).

The Contacts entry closes the sheet with the same state change as its cross, cancels a pending sheet and preserves the filter and selection. - ✅ Keyboard of the Conversations tabs and Ctrl+W ([#651](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/651), human request of 2026-10-21, validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-21; `ui/chat/keys.rs`, `ui/close_key.rs`): with the keyboard in the Conversations window, Alt + Up or Left shows the conversation above in the list, Alt + Down or Right the one below, round the list, in every chat style (LLFloaterIMContainer::handleKeyHere, LLTabContainer::selectNextTab; Contacts is left out, it is not a conversation); elsewhere these keys stay the camera's. Ctrl+W closes the frontmost window as File › Close Window does (LLFileCloseWindow, LLFloater::closeFrontmostFloater), taken before a text field (egui's fields used it to erase a word), not while a dialog is open, once per press; on Conversations it closes the conversation shown and shows the one below (above at the end of the list, LLTabContainer::removeTabPanel), and the window on the nearby chat or Contacts (LLMultiFloater::closeDockedFloater). A difference from the Legacy Viewer: minimized, Conversations closes as a window instead of closing a conversation not in sight.
- ✅ Button back to the latest messages ([#558](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/558), validated by the human in demo on 2026-10-21; `ui/chat/latest.rs`, demo `AURORA_DEMO_CONV_SCROLL`): once the reader scrolls a conversation away from its end (more than half the height in sight, or as soon as a message comes in below), a button fades in over the bottom of the lines, above the field, in every chat style: "Jump to the latest messages", or "3 new messages" counting others' messages that came in since (not ours nor notices); a click brings the end back, where the lines follow new messages again. Aurora only: the Legacy Viewer's history follows the end only while it is there (FSChatHistory::appendMessage).
