- ✅ Anti-spam (#678; task `anti-spam`): disabled-by-default flood protection for inventory/teleport offers, script prompts and sounds, following NACLAntiSpamQueue::checkEntry and the incoming IM/script/audio handlers. Session-local source/category blocks, separate or combined queues, own-object option, 10 events per 4 seconds and sound/preload multipliers of 6; per-source unblock and reset in Preferences > Protections, EN/FR. Preserve preload object/owner IDs, protect against rotating sound sources and bound active counters to 8,192 pairs with an overflow fallback; cache and virtualize the block list. Changing settings clears session counters/blocks. Keep sound thresholds identical at startup and after editing (the explicit multiplier only, avoiding setAllQueueAmounts' extra edit-only x5). Nil sound-stop commands always pass; existing object sound snapshots respect established blocks. Full `scripts/check.ps1` green: 4,251 passed, zero failures, 26 ignored; scripts, formatting and Clippy clean. Sixteen anti-spam regressions, including native reset/unblock clicks. `AURORA_DEMO_ANTI_SPAM=1/off/global` native FR/EN captures inspected: 5/0/4 blocked pairs, exit 0, no logged warnings/errors; settled 119.9/120.0/119.9 fps at the 120 fps cap (8.34 ms/frame in each). Human gift test exposed the self-owner exemption: scripted gifts identify their owner UUID, so Include my own objects now also counts our scripted gifts, while self text stays exempt (deliberate improvement over the Legacy Viewer). Added the separate nearby-chat event queue and the Legacy Viewer single-text LF limit: default 70, UI range 5–1,000, strict excess, shared IM block for chat/IM newline floods, saved-IM coverage and reference source exemptions. New `text` demo: four own gifts admit two notifications, object chat/resident IM with six LF exceed the test limit of five, three blocked pairs and one notice each. Native FR/EN captures inspected, exit 0, zero warnings/errors, 120.0/119.9 fps at the 120 fps cap (8.34 ms/frame). At maintainer request, Anti-spam now lives in a top-level Protections preferences page with the bundled shield icon; Privacy and Protections resets are independent. Block notices, demo navigation and the test guide point to Protections. Full checks remain green; native FR/EN Protections captures inspected, exit 0, no warnings/errors, 120.0 fps at the 120 fps cap (8.33/8.34 ms). Validated by the maintainer on 2026-10-21, conditional on disabled-by-default protection; confirmed both in defaults and old-settings deserialization. Merge authorized. Rebased onto the preferences redesign: the Protections page uses Cx declarations, participates in global search (anti-spam aliases) and modified settings, and resets independently of Privacy. The full updated workspace passes 4,251 tests, zero failures, 26 ignored.

- ✅ Import preview: relief of normal maps flipped on one axis (PBR material and mesh). The tangent frame of `preview.wgsl` was not divided by its determinant and the green channel was not inverted: with ordinary UVs the bumps were lit from below (Y flipped), with mirrored UVs or on the back of a double-sided face from the wrong side (X flipped). It now takes up the construction of `perturb_normal` (sign of the determinant included, +Y towards the top of the image like materialF.glsl), and the back of a double-sided face inverts the whole normal like pbropaqueF.glsl. The normalisation floor also flattened the relief of models that are small on screen (material sphere clearly attenuated): it only keeps the faces without UVs. GPU test `normal_map_bumps_face_the_light` (dome lit on the side of the light: ordinary UVs, mirrored, back). Validated in demo by the human on 2026-10-13 (material sphere with domes, before / after)

## Audio and voice

- ✅ Mute when minimized ([#1393](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1393); PR [#1443](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/pull/1443); task `mute-minimized-1393`; validated by the human in the online test viewer on 2026-10-29, merge authorized): retain the native minimized state while a frame owns the window; propagate the transient master mute to media plugins, chat videos and microphone transmission, following `LLViewerWindow::handleActivate` / `audio_update_volume`. Restore saved channel gains and the microphone toggle on return. Offline override: `AURORA_DEMO_MINIMIZED_MUTE=1|off` with `AURORA_DEMO_SOUND=1`.   - ✅ Human validation accepted for the delivered fix and merge authorized on 2026-10-28, build `0.8.2-beta.20261028.1+3.gd35291f59`. Offline startup checks: `AURORA_DEMO_CALL=group` and `AURORA_DEMO_LAND=sound`; native captures inspected.
- ✅ Crackle in the wind while flying (human report, 2026-10-28; task `wind-clicks`, no issue; [PR #1370](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/pull/1370); validated by the human on the grid on 2026-10-28): the wind fizzed with high-frequency crackles. A recording of the output showed impulses only at the seams between audio buffers: at the end of each buffer the generator synthesised one more sample, had no frame left to write it to and dropped it, the filter a step ahead of the output, so the sound skipped one sample about a hundred times a second. LLWindGen::windGenerate synthesises only while frames remain (`while (numsamples)`), on the even buffers of FMOD; ours stops before synthesising past the buffer and carries an interpolation step that a buffer ends inside (the 44.1 kHz resampler asks odd buffers) into the next one, so a sound is the same whatever the buffers. Recorded in the demo (`AURORA_DEMO_WIND=8,3,fly`, 30 s, 44.1 kHz device): 864 high-frequency impulses before, none after; the band above 5 kHz from -53.8 to -62.1 dB under the wind.
  - While looking, the output side: the device callbacks ran at normal priority (cpal without its `realtime` feature) over a single 10 ms period; under a CPU load one waited 47.6 ms, a cut. Now cpal puts the output and capture callbacks in the multimedia scheduler's "Audio" task (audio_thread_priority, MPL-2.0) and the WASAPI output queues 40 ms (the Legacy Viewer's FMOD buffers about 90 ms): the same load gives at most 11 ms between callbacks. The output thread logs, every 5 s, the callbacks that came after the queued sound ran out (warning) and the longest wait and mix (debug), which told the two causes apart on the grid.

Loops stop and resume across borders; attachments use the wearer and ridden vehicles keep their parcel exception without bypassing sound radius. - ✅ Media streams backup: import / export the saved music streams ([#1120](https://github.com/Aurora-Viewer/Aurora-Viewer/issues/1120), task `stream-list-backup`, validated by the human on 2026-10-26): Advanced › Media streams backup › Import / Export the stream list, the Legacy Viewer's FSStreamListImportXML / FSStreamListExportXML (llviewermenu.cpp). The file is its FSStreamList setting as pretty LLSD XML (`stream_list.xml`, a map with the URLs under "audio" and "version" 1, `settings/stream_list.rs`), so files go both ways between the two viewers; `aurora_llsd::to_pretty_xml_string` ports LLSDXMLFormatter's pretty form byte for byte. The dialogs run off the frame (`app/stream_list.rs`); a notice tells the file written or the number of streams imported. Deliberate difference: a file that is not an LLSD map is refused with a notice, where the Legacy Viewer would replace its list with it (an empty list after a wrong pick); blank and repeated URLs are skipped, as the Sound tab's Add never saves them.

The history window ("Recently played"): newest first, Ctrl / Shift to choose, Copy to clipboard one title per line, Clear, Max. entries (0 to 10,000). Better than the Legacy Viewer: a long title slides smoothly instead of losing one letter at a time (same 4 s wait at its end), and a smaller maximum applies at once. Aurora addition (human request on #1124): a text-only look (the window's title bar button, `audio.stream_title_text_only`): the title alone over the world with a shadow, dragged anywhere, its place kept with the windows'; its right click opens the history, copies the title, gives the window back or closes it. ## Netcode audit — 2026-10-13

- ✅ **2. Control changes sent at once (alignment with Firestorm).** A key press or release was written to a shared structure that the session read on a 25 ms tick, and a change was only sent when 100 ms had passed since the last AgentUpdate: while walking, turning or moving the camera the updates already flowed at 10 per second, so a press or a release waited up to 125 ms (measured on Agni: 94 ms at the median on a 202 ms ping, item 1); a press and its release inside one window were never sent, and a release sent late made the avatar overshoot. Fixed like `send_agent_update` (llviewermessage.cpp; `agent_update.rs`): any change of the control flags leaves at once and is confirmed by one more update (`control_flags_follow_up`); camera position past 0.01 m, camera axis past 0.2 × π / 360, body rotation past a dot of 0.9997, head rotation past 0.9997 at once or, down to 0.99999, once it has waited (threshold growing over 0.5 s), render state and flags: 125 updates a second at most (`MAX_AGENT_UPDATES_PER_SECOND`); one update a second keeps the agent alive. The viewer hands the session each change of the flags, in order, and whatever moved past those thresholds (`NetClient::set_controls` → `NetCommand::AgentControls`), so the session wakes for it instead of waiting for its tick, and a key tapped between two ticks is sent as two updates; the tick keeps the keep-alive, the follow-up and the head band. The draw distance no longer causes an update of its own: it travels with the next one, as in Firestorm. `check.ps1` green (1,833 tests passed, 23 ignored); tests of the rule: key at once and confirmed, tap shorter than the limit, thresholds, 125 a second for a camera moving at 1,000 frames a second, head band, keep-alive and forced update. Validated by the human on Agni on 2026-10-13 ("Test agent-update-now", 86 s of walking, stops, turns and short taps): input delay 0.0 ms in each of the 38 seconds that had a key change (before: 94 ms median, 125 at most), AgentUpdates 27 a second at the median and 121 at most (before: 10), no resend, no sequence gap.
- ✅ **7. Receive and send buffers of the UDP socket (alignment with Firestorm).** The socket was bound with the system's sizes (64 KiB on Windows). On arrival the main region and up to eight neighbours send their objects at once: whatever does not fit while the loop is busy is lost, resent a second later when it was reliable, gone for the unreliable position updates (jump at the next one). Done: 800,000 bytes in reception and 200,000 in emission, as Firestorm's `start_net` (`RECEIVE_BUFFER_SIZE`, `SEND_BUFFER_SIZE`, llmessage/net.cpp), through `socket2` (already there as a dependency of tokio); the sizes obtained are logged once per session (`udp socket buffers: …`, docs/TESTING.md, "Profiling › Network"). A system may grant less (Linux caps at `net.core.rmem_max`); a refusal is a warning and the session goes on. Test: the socket gets what is asked for on Windows, never less than its default elsewhere. `check.ps1` green (1,870 tests passed, 24 ignored). Verified on Agni by the human on 2026-10-13 ("Test udp-buffers-cache-io", two sessions): `receive 800000 bytes (system default 65536), send 200000 bytes (system default 65536)`. Its effect on losses does not show in these sessions: the loop was never busy when packets came in numbers, and the sessions before had no sequence gap either; the 3 and 12 gaps of the two sessions fell in seconds where the loop stayed under 0.2 ms, so they are losses on the link.

## Transparent picking and hover — PR #212

- ✅ Build inspector ownership status (human request, 2026-10-19): keep the owner first for editable, non-modifiable and locked selections; show another owner's resolved avatar/group name, and use “Non modifiable” in French. Follow `LLSelectMgr::selectGetOwner` for common versus multiple owners. Full checks green: 3,890 passed, 26 ignored; formatting and Clippy clean. Regressions cover name lookup without cached properties, group and multiple owners, and actual header placement for own/foreign selections in every permission state. Three French native captures inspected. Validated by the human in the test viewer on 2026-10-19; merge authorized.
- ✅ Blend hover (13/10): left and right clicks validated by the human; hover now stops at foreground Blend geometry at every opacity, even without a click action. The media hover shares the cached surface point; candidate bounds, per-face modes and large-face triangle hierarchies are retained. Mask holes remain pass-through. Full checks before integration: 1,795 passed, 23 ignored, Clippy green; sequential tests after three render-thread timing failures in parallel, all passing in isolation. Tests cover the cursor, the point bounding media hover, cache reuse, mixed materials and overrides on omitted faces. Demo `blend-zero/faint/half/solid-ready`: prim 976 without an action, Default cursor with no bitmap; before, Buy 971 was announced behind zero-alpha Blend. `mask-zero-ready` reaches Buy 971 with its bitmap. The pointer is held on target for the frame-650 sample; AURORA_HOVER_CHECK reported no difference. Human validation and merge authorization on 13/10/2026. Checks after integration with the updated main: 1,808 passed, 23 ignored, fmt and Clippy green.
- ✅ Left click on Blend (13/10): Blend triangles stop the press at every texel opacity, including zero, even without an action. Parallel candidate search resolves the material mode per face, so Blend elsewhere on a mixed-material object cannot fill a Mask hole. GPU picking retains zero-alpha Blend texels and the existing Mask threshold; posed surfaces also block media, avatar and action fallbacks behind them. This priority was requested by the human, unlike Firestorm's preference for a visible interactive object behind a non-actionable transparent surface. Full checks: 1,795 passed, 23 ignored; TE, PBR, legacy materials, zero alpha, occlusion, mixed faces and omitted-face overrides. Demo `blend-zero/faint/half/solid` reaches panel 976 without opening Buy 971; `mask-zero` still opens Buy. No GPU error in the inspected captures; zero-alpha Blend measured 8.34 ms per frame before / after in the small scene capped at 120 fps. Transparent right click, invisible selection, IGNORE and linked Sit rechecked. Clicks and hover validated by the human on 13/10.
- ✅ Transparent object selection (13/10): right click and left click in edit mode include transparent surfaces without a Touch handler or click action, as LLToolPie::handleRightMouseDown / LLToolSelectRect::handleMouseDown with pick_transparent. Only candidate triangles are tested, in parallel; displayed depth still occludes CPU geometry, IGNORE is traversed outside build mode and selectable in edit mode. Objects with no drawn faces remain indexed when CPU geometry exists. Checks: 1,792 passed, 23 ignored, Clippy green; TE / texture / PBR / legacy, sky, range, occlusion and overlapping bounds. Demo `transparent-menu/build` and `invisible-menu/build`: prim 976 selected in all four cases, instead of the object behind. Captures inspected with no GPU error; 120 fps retained (8.33–8.34 ms per frame). IGNORE, Disabled and linked Sit rechecked. Left / right clicks and hover validated by the human on 13/10.
